# Operator questions — PlantLibrary master rework (register of record)

*Opened 2026-10-13 at `R-0` by the orchestrating session (`PROMPT_master-rework-kickoff_2026-10-13.md` §2 rule 7).
A question never blocks the program: a step that depends on one is marked `waits Q-…` in the runbook's §3 and
skipped until the answer is recorded here verbatim with its date; every other step proceeds. The operator session
(runbook §O) walks the open rows in dependency order, records the answers verbatim, flips the state and commits.
The runbook's §Q mirrors this table; this file is the register of record. Rows are written by the orchestrator (from the kickoff, the audits, or a lane's proposed rows); a lane never writes this file. A † in an Options cell marks a recommendation the orchestrator refined at `R-3` where lanes disagreed or added evidence.*

## How to read a row

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ID | `Q-<lane>-<nn>`; `G` is the program lane (seeded by the kickoff prompt §4), `A1`…`A9`, `B1`…`B8`, `C1` the lanes that raise one |
| Question | What the operator decides, in plain words |
| Options | `(a)` is always the recommendation; others follow |
| Why (a) | The reasoning for the recommendation, short |
| Waits | The runbook steps marked `waits Q-…` on this row |
| Source | The audit row or step that raised it (what to read before answering) |
| When | The latest step it must be answered before; the ordering key of the operator session |
| State | `open` · `answered` · `withdrawn (<why>)` · `superseded (<Q id>)` (folded into another row; never asked) |
| Answer (verbatim, date) | The operator's words, unedited, in quotes, with the date and UTC time and how it was asked (`§O session <name>` or `orchestrator chat`), and the option it resolves to; `(a)` alone only when the operator said exactly that; a one-line answer for many rows is quoted in each row it resolves |
| Reading | Empty unless the answer departs from the options; then the orchestrator's reading and the step it inserts |

| `Q-G-11` | A new lane for a garden planner proposal (2D garden drawing, setup/edit mode, element library, areas with plant amounts, drag-and-drop placement, hover and click to the plant page) with HTML mockups and an anchoring strategy for the Dashboard and PyApp; Android excluded. | the operator's directive; options are the lane's design: (a) lane `B9` in the Workspace `docs/proposals/` set folder, five steps (analysis → mockups ∥ proposal → audit and gate `G-gp-1` in person → packages planned per suite after approval), building on `PROPOSAL_garden-designer_2026-07-10.md` | The operator asked for it in parallel through new sessions, anchored in the runbook | `B9-1`…`B9-5` | the operator in chat, 2026-10-14 | now | answered | "I want you to queue a new lane in the master runbook. I want to work out a proposal including html mockups for the garden and plant creation. It should be possible to create a new garden, draw e.g. beds, paths, hills and more in a well designed 2D drawing and place plants from the library to the garden (e.g. via drag and drop). The plants should be then shown in the garden. To create the garden, a garden setup and edit mode should be available with some default graphics to use, e.g. for stones, trees and more with options to rotate and scale the graphical elements and place them in the garden. Areas should be definable in the garden, e.g. bed south, lawn,... that are selectable and when plants are placed in these areas, they should be shown in the garden list structure as well with their amount. The placed plants should be selectable and hoverable to get information about the plants and when clicked the plant page should be opened. Think about everything that is required to draw the garden with much detail and how to anchor it in the dashboard and python app. For android we dont need to support this. Look at the following example images of garden drawings: C:\Temp\Garden_Manager For Reference look at the gardena garden planner or this planner: https://gardenplanner.net/ A detailed plan should be created for this including html mockups and a strategy on how to anchor it into the program after approval of the mockups. Queue this in parallel by using new sessions and anchoring it into the master runbook." — 2026-10-14, the operator in the orchestrator's chat; resolves to (a): lane B9 added to the runbook §3 (table), §3.2, §3.10, §3a and §L; `B9-1` launched the same day | the gate G-gp-1 is the operator's in person: the standing gate answer Q-G-10 does not cover it |
